=== PROMPT VARIANT ===

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer array nums.
You can do the following operation on the array at most once:

Choose any integer x such that nums remains non-empty on removing all occurrences of x.
Remove all occurrences of x from the array.

Return the maximum subarray sum across all possible resulting arrays.
A subarray is a contiguous non-empty sequence of elements within an array.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [-3,2,-2,-1,3,-2,3]
Output: 7
Explanation:
We can have the following arrays after at most one operation:

The original array is nums = [-3, 2, -2, -1, 3, -2, 3]. The maximum subarray sum is 3 + (-2) + 3 = 4.
Deleting all occurences of x = -3 results in nums = [2, -2, -1, 3, -2, 3]. The maximum subarray sum is 3 + (-2) + 3 = 4.
Deleting all occurences of x = -2 results in nums = [-3, 2, -1, 3, 3]. The maximum subarray sum is 2 + (-1) + 3 + 3 = 7.
Deleting all occurences of x = -1 results in nums = [-3, 2, -2, 3, -2, 3]. The maximum subarray sum is 3 + (-2) + 3 = 4.
Deleting all occurences of x = 3 results in nums = [-3, 2, -2, -1, -2]. The maximum subarray sum is 2.

The output is max(4, 4, 7, 4, 2) = 7.

Example 2:

Input: nums = [1,2,3,4]
Output: 10
Explanation:
It is optimal to not perform any operations.

 
Constraints:

1 <= nums.length <= 10^5
-10^6 <= nums[i] <= 10^6

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxSubarraySum(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, can we compute all these in O(n log n) by updating positions of each value to 0 and reverting? Yes! We can maintain a segment tree that supports point updates and returns max subarray sum of the current array. Initially current array = original nums. For each distinct value v:
- We need array with positions of v set to 0. Starting from original, we can update all positions where nums[i]==v to 0, query global max subarray sum, then revert those positions to original values.
Total point updates across all values: each position is updated to 0 once and reverted once when its value is processed. So O(n log n) if we process each distinct value once. But wait if we process values one by one and revert, each position updated twice per distinct value? No, for value v, update all occurrences of v to 0. Then after query, revert them to original. Next value w, update occurrences of w to 0. Since previous reverted, current array is original. So each position is updated to 0 exactly once (when its value processed) and reverted exactly once. Total 2n point updates. O(n log n). This is simple! Need implement segment tree for max subarray sum with point updates.

Segment tree details:
For each node representing segment, store:
- sum: total sum of segment
- pref: maximum prefix sum (non-empty? Usually can be 0? Need decide)
- suff: maximum suffix sum
- best: maximum subarray sum (non-empty)
For max subarray sum with non-empty, we can store pref/suff/best allowing empty? Let's define standard for non-empty:
For leaf with value x:
sum = x
pref = suff = best = x
Combine left L, right R:
sum = L.sum + R.sum
pref = max(L.pref, L.sum + R.pref)
suff = max(R.suff, R.sum + L.suff)
best = max(L.best, R.best, L.suff + R.pref)
This works for all negative values, returns max element. If leaf value 0, returns 0. Good.

But wait, the operation is "remove all occurrences of x" not "replace with 0". We argued equivalence. Need ensure maximum subarray sum of zeroed array with non-empty subarray equals maximum subarray sum after deletion, except for zero-only. Let's formalize.

Let A be original. For value x, let Z be A with x positions set to 0. Let D be A with x positions removed.

Claim: For any non-empty subarray of D with sum S, there exists a non-empty subarray of Z with sum S. Because D subarray corresponds to a contiguous sequence of original elements not equal to x. In Z, take the interval from the first to last original index of that sequence; it includes the same non-x elements plus possibly x elements between them, which are 0 in Z. Its sum is S. If the D subarray is a single element, interval is that element. So max_Z >= max_D.

Conversely, for any non-empty subarray of Z with sum S > 0? Or if it contains at least one non-x element, removing x positions from the interval yields a non-empty subarray of D with same sum S. If the Z subarray contains only x positions (all zeros), sum 0, no corresponding D subarray. Thus every positive sum subarray in Z corresponds to D. Therefore, if max_Z > 0, max_D = max_Z? Wait what if max_Z = positive, yes there is a positive subarray with non-x element, so corresponding D subarray. So max_D >= max_Z. Combined max_Z >= max_D? Need if max_D positive, max_Z >= max_D. If max_D negative, max_Z might be 0 invalid. But if max_Z positive, max_D positive equal. If max_Z <=0, max_D could be negative or 0? Let's examine. If max_Z <=0, then all non-x elements? Actually if there is any positive non-x element, max_Z positive. So if max_Z <=0, all non-x elements <=0 and max_Z is max(0, max non-x element) (because zeros at x positions). Then max_D = max non-x element (could be negative or 0 if actual zero non-x). If max_Z=0, max_D could be 0 if there is a non-x zero, or negative if no non-x zero. But if original_max >0, global max positive, so we don't rely on max_D for that x. But for computing max over x, if original_max >0, the global answer positive, so any x with max_Z<=0 won't be optimal. Thus taking max of original_max and max_Z over all x gives correct positive answer. If original_max <=0, we return original_max. So algorithm correct.

But wait: What if original_max >0, but for some x, max_Z is positive but corresponds to a subarray in Z that includes x positions and non-x elements; after removing x positions, the non-x elements become contiguous. Yes.

Let's design iterative segment tree for max subarray sum.

Iterative segment tree:
size = 1
while size < n: size <<= 1
sum_arr = [0]*(2*size)
pref = [-INF]*(2*size)
suff = [-INF]*(2*size)
best = [-INF]*(2*size)
For i in range(n):
    val = nums[i]
    pos = size + i
    sum_arr[pos] = pref[pos] = suff[pos] = best[pos] = val
For i in range(size-1, 0, -1):
    pull(i)

pull(i):
    l=i*2; r=l+1
    sum_arr[i] = sum_arr[l] + sum_arr[r]
    pref[i] = max(pref[l], sum_arr[l] + pref[r])
    suff[i] = max(suff[r], sum_arr[r] + suff[l])
    best[i] = max(best[l], best[r], suff[l] + pref[r])

Point update:
def update(pos, val):
    idx = size + pos
    sum_arr[idx] = pref[idx] = suff[idx] = best[idx] = val
    idx //= 2
    while idx:
        pull(idx)
        idx //= 2

Need process distinct values. We can group positions by value. Use dict mapping value -> list of indices. Since values can be negative, dict fine. Build groups:
from collections import defaultdict
groups = defaultdict(list)
for i, val in enumerate(nums):
    groups[val].append(i)

Compute original_max = best[1] after build. If original_max <= 0: return original_max. Wait if original_max =0 and there are positive? impossible. If original_max <=0, return original_max. But what if original_max is 0 and there is no actual zero? Original max 0 cannot happen if all negative. So 0 means at least one zero. Return 0. Good.

If original_max > 0:
ans = original_max
for val, indices in groups.items():
    # set positions to 0
    for idx in indices:
        update(idx, 0)
    if best[1] > ans: ans = best[1]
    # revert
    for idx in indices:
        update(idx, nums[idx])
return ans

But wait: If original_max > 0, do we need to process value v that is not in array? No operation original already included. If choose x not in array, no change, original. So no.

Complexity O(n log n + total updates log n) = O(n log n). Actually total updates = 2n, each update O(log n). Build O(n). Good.

Now, could deleting a positive value improve by removing it and allowing two negative blocks? Let's think if there is a more direct O(n) using prefix minima for each value? Not needed.

Potential issue with segment tree identity and `pref` for root if n=0? n>=1. Good.

Let's write code carefully.

```python
from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def maxSubarraySum(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        # build segment tree
        size = 1
        while size < n:
            size <<= 1
        NEG = -10**30
        total = [0]*(2*size)
        pref = [NEG]*(2*size)
        suff = [NEG]*(2*size)
        best = [NEG]*(2*size)
        
        for i, v in enumerate(nums):
            p = size + i
            total[p] = pref[p] = suff[p] = best[p] = v
        
        def pull(idx):
            l = idx << 1
            r = l | 1
            total[idx] = total[l] + total[r]
            pref[idx] = pref[l] if pref[l] >= total[l] + pref[r] else total[l] + pref[r]
            suff[idx] = suff[r] if suff[r] >= total[r] + suff[l] else total[r] + suff[l]
            best[idx] = max(best[l], best[r], suff[l] + pref[r])
        
        for idx in range(size-1, 0, -1):
            pull(idx)
        
        original_max = best[1]
        if original_max <= 0:
            return original_max
        
        groups = defaultdict(list)
        for i, v in enumerate(nums):
            groups[v].append(i)
        
        def update(pos, val):
            idx = size + pos
            total[idx] = pref[idx] = suff[idx] = best[idx] = val
            idx >>= 1
            while idx:
                pull(idx)
                idx >>= 1
        
        ans = original_max
        for v, positions in groups.items():
            for pos in positions:
                update(pos, 0)
            if best[1] > ans:
                ans = best[1]
            for pos in positions:
                update(pos, nums[pos])
        return ans
```

Need check `pull` for identity leaves: If both children identity, total=0, pref=NEG, suff=NEG, best=NEG. For internal nodes that combine identity and real, works. But in `pull`, `pref[idx] = max(pref[l], total[l] + pref[r])`. If l identity, total[l]=0, pref[r] real, max(NEG, pref[r]) = pref[r]. Good. If r identity, total[l]+NEG = NEG, max(pref[l], NEG) = pref[l]. Good. `suff`: if l identity, total[r]+NEG = NEG, max(suff[r], NEG)=suff[r]. If r identity, max(NEG, total[r]+suff[l]) = suff[l]. Good. `best`: if one identity, suff[l]+pref[r] might be NEG + something = NEG, max with real best. Good.

But wait: For an internal node with left identity and right real, `total[idx] = 0 + real_sum = real_sum`. `pref` = real_pref. `suff` = real_suff. `best` = real_best. Good. For left real and right identity, `pref` = real_pref, `suff` = real_suff, `best` = real_best. Good.

Now, what about `pref` and `suff` for a segment that contains real elements and padding identity at the end? Since padding is at the right end, internal nodes may combine real left and identity right. The segment tree root covers exactly size elements, including padding. `best[1]` should be max subarray sum over real elements only, because padding identity doesn't contribute. Good.

Now, let's consider if we should use `max` built-in in pull for speed. We can use if statements to avoid function call overhead. Current pull uses `max` for best and if for pref/suff. Could use `max` for all, but function call overhead in 3.4M pulls maybe okay but can optimize. Let's write efficient pull:

```python
def pull(i):
    l = i << 1
    r = l | 1
    sl = total[l]
    sr = total[r]
    total[i] = sl + sr
    pr = pref[r]
    pl = pref[l]
    pref[i] = pl if pl >= sl + pr else sl + pr
    su = suff[r]
    su_l = suff[l]
    suff[i] = su if su >= sr + su_l else sr + su_l
    b = best[l]
    br = best[r]
    cross = su_l + pr
    if br > b:
        b = br
    if cross > b:
        b = cross
    best[i] = b
```

This avoids max function. Good.

Need be careful variable names: `su` for suff[r], `su_l` for suff[l]. `pr` pref[r]. Good.

Now, update function:

```python
def set_leaf(pos, val):
    idx = size + pos
    total[idx] = pref[idx] = suff[idx] = best[idx] = val
    idx >>= 1
    while idx:
        l = idx << 1
        r = l | 1
        sl = total[l]; sr = total[r]
        total[idx] = sl + sr
        pl = pref[l]; pr = pref[r]
        pref[idx] = pl if pl >= sl + pr else sl + pr
        su = suff[r]; su_l = suff[l]
        suff[idx] = su if su >= sr + su_l else sr + su_l
        b = best[l]; br = best[r]
        cross = su_l + pr
        if br > b: b = br
        if cross > b: b = cross
        best[idx] = b
        idx >>= 1
```
